# Sun’s 28-witness construction

[All chapters](../README.md) · [Interactive explorer](../index.html?raw=1)

<a id="sun28-estimates"></a>

## Two height contradictions and the fixed multiplier

**Kernel checked.** With the compilation, completeness and height premises, the rational zero projection of a polynomial in 29 variables equals the integers. A fixed multiplier is given as a function of the three real height constants.

<a id="sun28-reciprocal"></a>

## A guarded nonzero rational root in one variable

**Kernel checked.** For coefficient lists of arbitrary length, the reciprocal equation has a rational zero exactly when both guards are nonzero and the retained monic polynomial has a nonzero rational root. Two fixed shifts cover zero roots.

<a id="sun28-specialization"></a>

## Exact finite branches at the fixed preparation data

**Kernel checked.** For the fixed data a single finite list recognizes the exact checked point relation at each of the 28 base coordinates. Its integer polynomial has the input and 28 rational witnesses. The integer projection still assumes simultaneous arithmetic witnesses, the residual height, the published S-unit bound and the ordinary chart.

Prerequisites · [A finite polynomial for the complete arithmetic certificate](08.md#sun28-concrete-atlas)

<details>
<summary>Lean declarations</summary>

- [`Sun28.Concrete.fixed_exact`](../../Lean/Topics/sun-28/concrete-atlas/FixedBranches.lean#L120)

</details>

<a id="sun28-ordinary"></a>

## Global height in the forward direction

**Header only.** The ordinary and scalar logarithmic estimates are proved on the specified curve. To infer integrality for every accepted input, the remaining specialized hypothesis is the five-mark height on the strict failure set of the least conductor test.

Prerequisites · [Integrality from the nine actual elliptic points](08.md#sun28-points-forward) · [The exponent-24 five-mark bound used by Sun](08.md#sun28-original-five-height) · [The local elliptic logarithm on the paper’s curve](08.md#sun28-local-logarithm)

<a id="sun28-definition"></a>

## Sun’s theorem on the ordinary integers

**Header only.** Sun’s fixed polynomial defines the integers assuming the pointwise scalar bound at surviving exceptional inputs, the ordinary bound off two explicit tests, the published inputs, the prime estimates and prescribed points. On concentrated inputs two explicit denominator barriers further restrict the conic cases.

Prerequisites · [The polynomial on the exceptional set](08.md#sun28-weighted-projection) · [Pointwise height at exceptional accepted inputs and the remaining ordinary tuples](08.md#sun28-remaining-point-height) · [Integer witnesses from prime tuples and paired twist points](08.md#sun28-joint)

<a id="sun28-generator-depth"></a>

## Infinite order by dyadic doubling

**Kernel checked.** On the displayed generator the reciprocal abscissa has dyadic order 3v(2). Each doubling increases parameter order by v(2). Thus the generator has infinite order and the odd-prime divisibility implication only needs formal comparison and the scalar contact hypotheses.

Prerequisites · [Divisibility on the actual elliptic generator](08.md#sun28-scalar-index)

<a id="sun28-contact-transport"></a>

## All-prime transport of the five projective depths

**Kernel checked.** The map (U,V) ↦ (2U+V,U) preserves every normalized depth of the five corresponding primitive forms at every prime. Its optimal multiplicative height factor is three. Changing the excluded set from S to E gives the explicit logarithmic height coefficient C times the 24th power of the product of primes in E minus S, plus log 3.

Prerequisites · [The exponent-24 five-mark bound used by Sun](08.md#sun28-original-five-height) · [Three products of projective contact primes](08.md#sun28-ordinary-products)

<a id="sun28-ordinary-heights"></a>

**Kernel checked.** On the three shared-slope detectors, the original five-mark bound, the ordinary chart and a three-scale inequality with coefficients c₁,c₂ give both exponents 3/8 and 3/2. **Kernel checked.** For the nine cyclic indices, the three accepted ordinary poles, the completed chart, the original five-mark estimate, the three-scale and scalar height comparisons, s−1=Mq with q nonzero, and z nonzero imply β=2X²+3=n and X∈ℤ. <a id="sun28-witness-indices"></a>

## Unique signed indices of the nine rational points

**Kernel checked.** The rational oddness chart accepts kP exactly for odd k. The nine doubled affine records have unique integer labels, with s−1=Mq and all the required translated point identities.

Prerequisites · [The doubled Mordell–Weil group](01.md#generator) · [The four quadratic-field square-root charts](08.md#sun28-square)

<a id="sun28-three-scales"></a>

## The heights of the three slopes bound all four indices

**Kernel checked.** For the actual abscissae of four odd multiples on Sun’s curve the three slope heights bounded by H≥1 imply B²≤3H/2+10. The index-to-product comparison allows a loss of 3 log 7.

Prerequisites · [Three products of projective contact primes](08.md#sun28-ordinary-products)

<a id="sun28-scalar-height"></a>

## The seven-leaf scalar pole gives a square-root height bound

**Kernel checked.** The signed scalar tree gives M₅(β)⁴⁸ dividing s−1 outside an explicit cutoff. The original height then bounds h(β) by the scalar coefficient times √|s−1|. For the explicit multiplier list the scalar-only coefficient is H*.

<a id="sun28-points-forward"></a>

## Integrality from the nine actual elliptic points

**Kernel checked.** The three ordinary poles and the signed scalar pole on the rationally encoded nine points give x=k and N=[2k²+3]P, from the original five-contact bound and the completed local chart. The shared cutoff and elliptic multiplier are independent of the input.

<a id="sun28-free-parameters"></a>

## The 27 free coordinates and the polynomial projection

**Kernel checked.** The 27 rational coordinates and the point tests determine each other up to the finite branch choices. For an actual guarded implementation, one-way decoding and integer branch witnesses suffice. The specific output polynomial defines the integers conditional on these obligations, the local chart and the large-residual height through the published unit theorem.

Prerequisites · [Integrality from the nine actual elliptic points](08.md#sun28-points-forward) · [Uniform projection of tested triangular systems](08.md#sun28-uniform) · [The original five marks and the rational two-unit theorem](08.md#sun28-five-mark-units) · [Five-mark height beyond all seven sufficient tests](08.md#sun28-large-even-support)

<a id="sun28-fixed-preparation"></a>

## Explicit common constants and the formal subgroup

<a id="sun28-concrete-atlas"></a>

## A finite polynomial for the complete arithmetic certificate

**Kernel checked.** For the fixed data a single finite list recognizes the exact checked point relation at each of the 28 base coordinates. Its integer polynomial has the input and 28 rational witnesses. The integer projection still assumes simultaneous arithmetic witnesses, the residual height, the published S-unit bound and the ordinary chart.

<a id="sun28-real-ratio"></a>

## A common index in an arithmetic progression

**Kernel checked.** A compact-group progression theorem supplies one signed index for both real evaluations and avoids any prescribed finite target families. With the parameter model and reciprocal bounds, all six scalar entries are at most one in absolute value. Construction of the concrete topological input and the simultaneous finite-place witnesses remain open.

<a id="sun28-least-conductor"></a>

## The least conductor of the ten unit equations

**Kernel checked.** Reducing both fractions on each of the ten marked triples and minimizing their conductors gives the height bound 192 n log(128n)² + 97 from published Corollary 7.2. The original height estimate is equivalent to its restriction to the strict failure of this test.

<a id="sun28-real-inputs"></a>

<a id="sun28-pole-realization"></a>

## From simultaneous rational points to the four poles

**Kernel checked.** Given the original paired-twist statement, a square reduced denominator, positivity, the real bound and depth four at the nine preparation primes imply the exact pole predicate. The fixed polynomial has the integer projection under the published unit bound, residual height, one simultaneous preparation and paired twists.

<a id="sun28-comparison-crt"></a>

**Kernel checked.** Given the two reduction orders and their explicit coprimality and dyadic hypotheses, all the signed comparison indices satisfying the four families of congruences form one residue class modulo the least common multiple. Each prescribed prime has exactly its required valuation.

<a id="sun28-selection-projection"></a>

<a id="sun28-finite-witness"></a>

<a id="sun28-elliptic-topology"></a>

**Kernel checked.** On a fixed norm fibre each sign excludes at most two residues. An odd prime divides both adjacent norms at one shift exactly at (0,y) with y²=−1. The resulting tests are equivalent to the original local restrictions.

<a id="sun28-prescribed-divisors"></a>

## Prescribing prime divisors on every admissible twist fibre

**Kernel checked.** Over each admissible first parameter there are arbitrarily large admissible second parameters, with any allowed finite set of split prime divisors and any disjoint finite avoidance condition, giving both points with the same n. The integral construction excludes prime outputs and an explicit rational construction reaches the prime 3697.

<a id="sun28-mixed-density"></a>

## Summable losses in the mixed Gaussian norm sieve

**Kernel checked.** For Sun’s at most thirteen cleared shifts the sum of normalized mixed losses over any finite set of primes at least 2810 is at most one half. At a prime outside the fixed modulus, the obstruction on an actual generator equals the finite-fibre test. A weighted union bound needs only three signed coordinate boxes per obstruction prime to obtain a lower bound, once the corresponding prime counts are supplied.

Prerequisites · [Residue tests for simultaneous adjacent Gaussian norms](08.md#sun28-local-norm-residues) · [The prime pattern required by the 28-witness proof](08.md#sun28-contact-patterns)

<a id="sun28-weighted-selection"></a>

<a id="sun28-five-pencils"></a>

<a id="sun28-deep-sector"></a>

## The logarithmic sector for each of the five contacts

**Kernel checked.** The odd valuations of the three quadratic characters occur zero or twice. Their product gives discriminant at most 64 T². The three published height inputs imply the original inequality whenever (1+log T)^4 times one contact logarithm is at most 625 T^23. The pure-power threshold T^22 suffices.

Prerequisites · [Five simultaneous Pell systems](08.md#sun28-five-pencils)

<a id="sun28-actual-twists"></a>

## Squareclasses at the three ordinary poles and the scalar pole

**Kernel checked.** At an ordinary pole the first class is five distinct primes in two specified rays, times 1 or 17. The scalar first class is the squarefree part of an explicit integer. The second class is locally square at the nine fixed primes and both classes lie on a rational norm conic. Two square conditions give simultaneous sections.

Prerequisites · [Prescribing prime divisors on every admissible twist fibre](08.md#sun28-prescribed-divisors) · [The fixed polynomial with three arithmetic inputs and two published inputs](08.md#sun28-sextic-projection)

<a id="sun28-sparse-height"></a>

**Kernel checked.** For each marked value Fi=d b² with squarefree d, divide its centered Pell equations by b². The effective S-integer estimate gives the original height coefficient when a marked value has at most four prime factors with product at most T⁴.

<a id="sun28-prime-summation"></a>

## From individual medium primes to their total weight

**Kernel checked.** A uniform unweighted upper bound at each prime up to the logarithmic cutoff gives the weighted collision estimate on the same rows. The square-reciprocal sum and the polynomial times exponential error are bounded explicitly.

<a id="sun28-ray-fractions"></a>

## The two-sign Gaussian fibre and its Euler product

**Kernel checked.** The base contributes 1/64 and every prescribed product of unit norm fibres has explicit lower fraction (exp(−48)/(64·(2(1+4log L))⁴))ᵏ. The exact dilation changes the quadratic coefficients by d².

The reverse implication follows from the real elliptic and quadric theorems, finite recurrence, fixed-class density, ordinary-prime transference, relative upper moments and the prescribed-point assertion.

<a id="sun28-lucas-allowance"></a>

## The signed Lucas power and the scalar height allowance

**Kernel checked.** When the Lucas equation holds, the second coordinate has log absolute value at least (c/4) log c for c=|s−1|≥12. With L=lcm(M,R⁴⁸), either H*R²⁴ or 2R⁴⁸ log R is at most L log L/24.

<a id="sun28-ordinary-indices"></a>

## The two ordinary index tests

**Kernel checked.** The first comparison involves only three indices and forces the Lucas discrepancy to vanish. On its zero locus the second comparison involves only two indices and bounds the absolute logarithm of the second Lucas coordinate. Relative heights give a sufficient alternative independent of the common scale on their left-hand side.

<a id="sun28-numerator-pencil"></a>

## Simultaneous Pell systems from the scalar numerator

**Kernel checked.** For x=a/b the identity β−3=2(a/b)² yields a pair of integral equations. Dividing by a square divisor u² of a² gives an S-integral system whose constants depend on a/u. The effective estimate applies when rad(|u|)≤T and rad(|u|)¹⁸(1+log T)⁴log|a/u|≤625T²³.

<a id="sun28-input-density"></a>

## Density of the exceptional rational inputs

**Kernel checked.** If E(B) counts reduced x of naive height at most B violating the original scalar estimate, then E(B)≤324BK for K≥1 and 2 log B+4≤(K+1)²⁴. Thus E(B)/B² tends to zero. The assertion concerns the original inequality at actual inputs.

<a id="level-relative-degree"></a>

**Kernel checked.** A labelled torsor of order 36 bounds the relative degree by 36. The absolute degree divides 36 times the coarse degree, which divides 16.

</details>

<a id="level-minus-one-degree"></a>

## Degree sixteen at the negative unit slope

**Kernel checked.** At slope −1 the coarse field has degree sixteen and does not contain a primitive cube root of unity. A full field containing the marking and such a root has degree divisible by 32.

<a id="uniform-marked-rank"></a>

## A proper marked factor gives an infinite-order point

**Kernel checked.** When the twist is squarefree and the mark and complementary factor are both greater than one, an actual marked point supplies a rational point of infinite order and eight distinct rational classes modulo doubling. Every admissible pair has unbounded first twists with this marked point. On a companion with eight local classes, the marked cover is soluble exactly when the rational rank is positive, under Mordell–Weil finite generation.

Prerequisites · [Unbounded common multipliers for fixed squareclasses](08.md#seeded-pair) · [A single companion point realizes the pair](08.md#seeded-companion-criterion) · [Positive rank and the marked fixed cover](08.md#fixed-rank-equivalence)

<a id="quartic-window-projection"></a>

## A polynomial theorem with a one-equation companion hypothesis

**Kernel checked.** On squarefree proper twists the finite covering test is equivalent to solubility of one affine quartic for the same multiplier. The polynomial theorem consumes this point and the window and geometric hypotheses.

Prerequisites · [The polynomial from the finite covering criterion](08.md#finite-label-projection)
